 117: #[derive(Clone)]
 118: pub(crate) struct SharedTunLifecycle {
 119:     inner: Arc<Mutex<Box<dyn TunLifecycle>>>,
 120: }
 121: 
 122: impl fmt::Debug for SharedTunLifecycle {
 123:     fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
 124:         f.write_str("SharedTunLifecycle(..)")
 125:     }
 126: }
 127: 
 128: impl SharedTunLifecycle {
 129:     pub(crate) fn new(inner: Box<dyn TunLifecycle>) -> Self {
 130:         Self {
 131:             inner: Arc::new(Mutex::new(inner)),
 132:         }
 133:     }
 134: 
 135:     pub(crate) fn prepare_and_open(
 136:         &self,
 137:         interface_name: &str,
 138:         context: &RuntimeContext,
 139:     ) -> Result<TunDevice, BackendError> {
 140:         self.with_lock("prepare_and_open", |inner| {
 141:             inner.prepare_and_open(interface_name, context)
 142:         })
 143:     }
 144: 
 145:     pub(crate) fn reconcile_routes(
 146:         &self,
 147:         interface_name: &str,
 148:         previous_routes: &[Route],
 149:         next_routes: &[Route],
 150:     ) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 151:         self.with_lock("reconcile_routes", |inner| {
 152:             inner.reconcile_routes(interface_name, previous_routes, next_routes)
 153:         })
 154:     }
 155: 
 156:     pub(crate) fn cleanup(&self, interface_name: &str) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 157:         self.with_lock("cleanup", |inner| inner.cleanup(interface_name))
 158:     }
 159: 
 160:     pub(crate) fn reconcile_exit_mode(
 161:         &self,
 162:         previous_mode: ExitMode,
 163:         next_mode: ExitMode,
 164:     ) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 165:         self.with_lock("reconcile_exit_mode", |inner| {
 166:             inner.reconcile_exit_mode(previous_mode, next_mode)
 167:         })
 168:     }
 169: 
 170:     fn with_lock<T>(
 171:         &self,
 172:         operation: &str,
 173:         action: impl FnOnce(&mut dyn TunLifecycle) -> Result<T, BackendError>,
 174:     ) -> Result<T, BackendError> {
 175:         let mut guard = self.inner.lock().map_err(|_| {
 176:             BackendError::internal(format!(
 177:                 "linux userspace-shared TUN lifecycle mutex poisoned during {operation}"
 178:             ))
 179:         })?;
 180:         action(guard.as_mut())
 181:     }
 182: }

 186:         &mut self,
 187:         interface_name: &str,

 189:     ) -> Result<TunDevice, BackendError>;
 190: 
 191:     fn reconcile_routes(
 192:         &mut self,
 193:         interface_name: &str,
 194:         previous_routes: &[Route],
 195:         next_routes: &[Route],
 196:     ) -> Result<(), BackendError>;
 197: 
 198:     fn reconcile_exit_mode(
 199:         &mut self,
 200:         previous_mode: ExitMode,

 203: 
 204:     fn cleanup(&mut self, interface_name: &str) -> Result<(), BackendError>;

 208: pub(crate) struct DirectTunLifecycle {
 209:     runner: LinuxCommandRunner,
 210: }
 211: 
 212: impl TunLifecycle for DirectTunLifecycle {
 213:     fn prepare_and_open(
 214:         &mut self,
 215:         interface_name: &str,
 216:         context: &RuntimeContext,
 217:     ) -> Result<TunDevice, BackendError> {
 218:         ParsedLocalCidr::parse(&context.local_cidr)?;
 219:         remove_interface_if_present(&mut self.runner, interface_name)?;
 220:         let result = (|| -> Result<TunDevice, BackendError> {
 221:             self.runner.run(
 222:                 "ip",
 223:                 &[
 224:                     "tuntap".to_string(),
 225:                     "add".to_string(),
 226:                     "dev".to_string(),
 227:                     interface_name.to_string(),
 228:                     "mode".to_string(),
 229:                     "tun".to_string(),
 230:                 ],
 231:             )?;
 232:             self.runner.run(
 233:                 "ip",
 234:                 &[
 235:                     "address".to_string(),
 236:                     "add".to_string(),
 237:                     context.local_cidr.clone(),
 238:                     "dev".to_string(),
 239:                     interface_name.to_string(),
 240:                 ],
 241:             )?;
 242:             self.runner.run(
 243:                 "ip",
 244:                 &[
 245:                     "link".to_string(),
 246:                     "set".to_string(),
 247:                     "up".to_string(),
 248:                     "dev".to_string(),
 249:                     interface_name.to_string(),
 250:                 ],
 251:             )?;
 252: 
 253:             let device = SyncDevice::open(interface_name).map_err(|err| {
 254:                 BackendError::internal(format!(
 255:                     "linux userspace-shared TUN create/open failed for {interface_name}: {err}"
 256:                 ))
 257:             })?;
 258:             device.set_nonblocking(true).map_err(|err| {
 259:                 BackendError::internal(format!(
 260:                     "linux userspace-shared TUN nonblocking setup failed for {interface_name}: {err}"
 261:                 ))
 262:             })?;
 263:             Ok(TunDevice::real(device))
 264:         })();
 265: 
 266:         if result.is_err() {
 267:             let _ = remove_interface_if_present(&mut self.runner, interface_name);
 268:         }
 269: 
 270:         result
 271:     }
 272: 
 273:     fn reconcile_routes(
 274:         &mut self,
 275:         interface_name: &str,
 276:         previous_routes: &[Route],
 277:         next_routes: &[Route],
 278:     ) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 279:         reconcile_backend_routes(
 280:             &mut self.runner,
 281:             interface_name,
 282:             previous_routes,
 283:             next_routes,
 284:         )
 285:     }
 286: 
 287:     fn reconcile_exit_mode(
 288:         &mut self,
 289:         previous_mode: ExitMode,
 290:         next_mode: ExitMode,
 291:     ) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 292:         reconcile_backend_exit_mode(&mut self.runner, previous_mode, next_mode)
 293:     }
 294: 
 295:     fn cleanup(&mut self, _interface_name: &str) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 296:         remove_interface_if_present(&mut self.runner, _interface_name)
 297:     }
 298: }

 424: 
 425: fn remove_interface_if_present(
 426:     runner: &mut dyn WireguardCommandRunner,
 427:     interface_name: &str,
 428: ) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 429:     let args = vec![
 430:         "link".to_string(),
 431:         "del".to_string(),
 432:         "dev".to_string(),
 433:         interface_name.to_string(),
 434:     ];
 435:     match runner.run("ip", &args) {
 436:         Ok(()) => Ok(()),
 437:         Err(err) if is_missing_interface_error(&err) => Ok(()),
 438:         Err(err) => Err(err),
 439:     }
 440: }

 442: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]
 443: pub(crate) struct TestTunLifecycle {
 444:     state: TunTestState,
 445:     behavior: TestTunBehavior,
 446: }
 447: 
 448: impl Default for TestTunLifecycle {
 449:     fn default() -> Self {
 450:         Self::new()
 451:     }
 452: }
 453: 
 454: impl TestTunLifecycle {
 455:     pub(crate) fn new() -> Self {
 456:         Self {
 457:             state: TunTestState::default(),
 458:             behavior: TestTunBehavior::Succeed,
 459:         }
 460:     }
 461: 
 462:     #[cfg_attr(not(test), allow(dead_code))]
 463:     pub(crate) fn with_behavior(behavior: TestTunBehavior) -> Self {
 464:         Self {
 465:             state: TunTestState::default(),
 466:             behavior,
 467:         }
 468:     }
 469: 
 470:     #[cfg_attr(not(test), allow(dead_code))]
 471:     pub(crate) fn state(&self) -> TunTestState {
 472:         self.state.clone()
 473:     }
 474: }
 475: 
 476: impl TunLifecycle for TestTunLifecycle {
 477:     fn prepare_and_open(
 478:         &mut self,
 479:         interface_name: &str,
 480:         context: &RuntimeContext,
 481:     ) -> Result<TunDevice, BackendError> {
 482:         self.state
 483:             .record_prepare(interface_name, &context.local_cidr);
 484:         match &self.behavior {
 485:             TestTunBehavior::Succeed => Ok(TunDevice::test_handle(self.state.clone())),
 486:             TestTunBehavior::FailBeforeOpen(message) => {
 487:                 Err(BackendError::internal(message.clone()))
 488:             }
 489:             TestTunBehavior::FailAfterOpen(message) => {
 490:                 drop(TunDevice::test_handle(self.state.clone()));
 491:                 Err(BackendError::internal(message.clone()))
 492:             }
 493:         }
 494:     }
 495: 
 496:     fn reconcile_routes(
 497:         &mut self,
 498:         interface_name: &str,
 499:         previous_routes: &[Route],
 500:         next_routes: &[Route],
 501:     ) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 502:         validate_interface_name(interface_name)?;
 503:         validate_route_set(next_routes)?;
 504:         self.state.record_route_reconcile(interface_name);
 505: 
 506:         let previous_programmed = backend_programmed_route_cidrs(previous_routes);
 507:         let next_programmed = backend_programmed_route_cidrs(next_routes);
 508:         let route_behavior = self.state.route_behavior();
 509: 
 510:         let result = self.state.apply_route_reconciliation_for_test(
 511:             &previous_programmed,
 512:             &next_programmed,
 513:             &route_behavior,
 514:             true,
 515:         );
 516:         match result {
 517:             Ok(()) => Ok(()),
 518:             Err(err) => {
 519:                 let rollback_result = self.state.apply_route_reconciliation_for_test(
 520:                     &next_programmed,
 521:                     &previous_programmed,
 522:                     &route_behavior,
 523:                     false,
 524:                 );
 525:                 match rollback_result {
 526:                     Ok(()) => Err(err),
 527:                     Err(rollback_err) => Err(combine_route_reconciliation_error(err, rollback_err)),
 528:                 }
 529:             }
 530:         }
 531:     }
 532: 
 533:     fn reconcile_exit_mode(
 534:         &mut self,
 535:         previous_mode: ExitMode,
 536:         next_mode: ExitMode,
 537:     ) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 538:         self.state.record_exit_mode_reconcile(next_mode);
 539:         let exit_mode_behavior = self.state.exit_mode_behavior();
 540: 
 541:         let result = self
 542:             .state
 543:             .apply_exit_mode_plan_for_test(next_mode, &exit_mode_behavior, true);
 544:         match result {
 545:             Ok(()) => Ok(()),
 546:             Err(err) => {
 547:                 let rollback_result = self.state.apply_exit_mode_plan_for_test(
 548:                     previous_mode,
 549:                     &exit_mode_behavior,
 550:                     false,
 551:                 );
 552:                 match rollback_result {
 553:                     Ok(()) => Err(err),
 554:                     Err(rollback_err) => {
 555:                         Err(combine_exit_mode_reconciliation_error(err, rollback_err))
 556:                     }
 557:                 }
 558:             }
 559:         }
 560:     }
 561: 
 562:     fn cleanup(&mut self, interface_name: &str) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 563:         self.state.record_cleanup(interface_name);
 564:         self.state.clear_programmed_routes();
 565:         Ok(())
 566:     }
 567: }
 568: 
 569: #[allow(dead_code)]
 570: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]
 571: pub(crate) enum TestTunBehavior {
 572:     Succeed,
 573:     FailBeforeOpen(String),
 574:     FailAfterOpen(String),
 575: }
 576: 
 577: #[cfg_attr(not(test), allow(dead_code))]
 578: #[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Default)]
 579: pub(crate) enum TestRouteBehavior {
 580:     #[default]
 581:     Succeed,
 582:     FailOnReplace {
 583:         cidr: String,
 584:         message: String,
 585:     },
 586:     FailOnDelete {
 587:         cidr: String,
 588:         message: String,
 589:     },
 590: }
 591: 
 592: #[cfg_attr(not(test), allow(dead_code))]
 593: #[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Default)]
 594: pub(crate) enum TestExitModeBehavior {
 595:     #[default]
 596:     Succeed,
 597:     FailOnDeleteTable {
 598:         message: String,
 599:     },
 600:     FailOnDeletePriority {
 601:         message: String,
 602:     },
 603:     FailOnAddPriority {
 604:         message: String,
 605:     },
 606: }

 608: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
 609: pub(crate) struct TunTestState {
 610:     inner: Arc<Mutex<TunTestStateInner>>,
 611: }
 612: 
 613: impl TunTestState {
 614:     fn record_prepare(&self, interface_name: &str, local_cidr: &str) {
 615:         let mut inner = self.inner.lock().expect("tun test state mutex poisoned");
 616:         inner.prepare_calls += 1;
 617:         inner.last_interface_name = Some(interface_name.to_string());
 618:         inner.last_local_cidr = Some(local_cidr.to_string());
 619:     }
 620: 
 621:     fn record_cleanup(&self, interface_name: &str) {
 622:         let mut inner = self.inner.lock().expect("tun test state mutex poisoned");
 623:         inner.cleanup_calls += 1;
 624:         inner.last_cleanup_interface_name = Some(interface_name.to_string());
 625:     }
 626: 
 627:     #[cfg_attr(not(test), allow(dead_code))]
 628:     pub(crate) fn set_next_recv_error(&self, message: impl Into<String>) {
 629:         let mut inner = self.inner.lock().expect("tun test state mutex poisoned");
 630:         inner.next_recv_error = Some(message.into());
 631:     }
 632: 
 633:     fn increment_live_handles(&self) {
 634:         let mut inner = self.inner.lock().expect("tun test state mutex poisoned");
 635:         inner.live_handles += 1;
 636:     }
 637: 
 638:     fn decrement_live_handles(&self) {
 639:         let mut inner = self.inner.lock().expect("tun test state mutex poisoned");
 640:         inner.live_handles = inner.live_handles.saturating_sub(1);
 641:     }
 642: 
 643:     fn record_route_reconcile(&self, interface_name: &str) {
 644:         let mut inner = self.inner.lock().expect("tun test state mutex poisoned");
 645:         inner.route_reconcile_calls += 1;
 646:         inner.last_route_interface_name = Some(interface_name.to_string());
 647:     }
 648: 
 649:     fn clear_programmed_routes(&self) {
 650:         let mut inner = self.inner.lock().expect("tun test state mutex poisoned");
 651:         inner.programmed_route_cidrs.clear();
 652:     }
 653: 
 654:     #[allow(dead_code)]
 655:     fn queue_inbound_packet(&self, packet: Vec<u8>) {
 656:         let mut inner = self.inner.lock().expect("tun test state mutex poisoned");
 657:         inner.queued_inbound_packets.push_back(packet);
 658:     }
 659: 
 660:     #[allow(dead_code)]
 661:     fn dequeue_inbound_packet(&self) -> Option<Vec<u8>> {
 662:         let mut inner = self.inner.lock().expect("tun test state mutex poisoned");
 663:         inner.queued_inbound_packets.pop_front()
 664:     }
 665: 
 666:     #[allow(dead_code)]
 667:     fn take_next_recv_error(&self) -> Option<String> {
 668:         let mut inner = self.inner.lock().expect("tun test state mutex poisoned");
 669:         inner.next_recv_error.take()
 670:     }
 671: 
 672:     #[allow(dead_code)]
 673:     fn record_outbound_packet(&self, packet: Vec<u8>) {
 674:         let mut inner = self.inner.lock().expect("tun test state mutex poisoned");
 675:         inner.recorded_outbound_packets.push(packet);
 676:     }
 677: 
 678:     #[allow(dead_code)]
 679:     fn recorded_outbound_packets(&self) -> Vec<Vec<u8>> {
 680:         let inner = self.inner.lock().expect("tun test state mutex poisoned");
 681:         inner.recorded_outbound_packets.clone()
 682:     }
 683: 
 684:     fn record_exit_mode_reconcile(&self, target_mode: ExitMode) {
 685:         let mut inner = self.inner.lock().expect("tun test state mutex poisoned");
 686:         inner.exit_mode_reconcile_calls += 1;
 687:         inner.last_exit_mode_target = Some(target_mode);
 688:     }
 689: 
 690:     fn route_behavior(&self) -> TestRouteBehavior {
 691:         let inner = self.inner.lock().expect("tun test state mutex poisoned");
 692:         inner.route_behavior.clone()
 693:     }
 694: 
 695:     #[cfg_attr(not(test), allow(dead_code))]
 696:     pub(crate) fn set_route_behavior(&self, behavior: TestRouteBehavior) {
 697:         let mut inner = self.inner.lock().expect("tun test state mutex poisoned");
 698:         inner.route_behavior = behavior;
 699:     }
 700: 
 701:     fn exit_mode_behavior(&self) -> TestExitModeBehavior {
 702:         let inner = self.inner.lock().expect("tun test state mutex poisoned");
 703:         inner.exit_mode_behavior.clone()
 704:     }
 705: 
 706:     #[cfg_attr(not(test), allow(dead_code))]
 707:     pub(crate) fn set_exit_mode_behavior(&self, behavior: TestExitModeBehavior) {
 708:         let mut inner = self.inner.lock().expect("tun test state mutex poisoned");
 709:         inner.exit_mode_behavior = behavior;
 710:     }
 711: 
 712:     fn apply_route_reconciliation_for_test(
 713:         &self,
 714:         previous_programmed: &[String],
 715:         next_programmed: &[String],
 716:         route_behavior: &TestRouteBehavior,
 717:         failures_enabled: bool,
 718:     ) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 719:         let mut inner = self.inner.lock().expect("tun test state mutex poisoned");
 720: 
 721:         for cidr in next_programmed {
 722:             if failures_enabled {
 723:                 maybe_fail_route_replace(route_behavior, cidr)?;
 724:             }
 725:             if !inner
 726:                 .programmed_route_cidrs
 727:                 .iter()
 728:                 .any(|existing| existing == cidr)
 729:             {
 730:                 inner.programmed_route_cidrs.push(cidr.clone());
 731:             }
 732:             inner.route_mutations.push(TunRouteMutation {
 733:                 kind: TunRouteMutationKind::Replace,
 734:                 destination_cidr: cidr.clone(),
 735:             });
 736:         }
 737: 
 738:         for cidr in previous_programmed {
 739:             if next_programmed.iter().any(|next| next == cidr) {
 740:                 continue;
 741:             }
 742:             if failures_enabled {
 743:                 maybe_fail_route_delete(route_behavior, cidr)?;
 744:             }
 745:             inner
 746:                 .programmed_route_cidrs
 747:                 .retain(|existing| existing != cidr);
 748:             inner.route_mutations.push(TunRouteMutation {
 749:                 kind: TunRouteMutationKind::Delete,
 750:                 destination_cidr: cidr.clone(),
 751:             });
 752:         }
 753: 
 754:         Ok(())
 755:     }
 756: 
 757:     fn apply_exit_mode_plan_for_test(
 758:         &self,
 759:         mode: ExitMode,
 760:         exit_mode_behavior: &TestExitModeBehavior,
 761:         failures_enabled: bool,
 762:     ) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 763:         let mut inner = self.inner.lock().expect("tun test state mutex poisoned");
 764: 
 765:         if failures_enabled {
 766:             maybe_fail_exit_mode_delete_table(exit_mode_behavior)?;
 767:         }
 768:         inner
 769:             .exit_mode_mutations
 770:             .push(TunExitModeMutation::DeleteTable);
 771: 
 772:         if failures_enabled {
 773:             maybe_fail_exit_mode_delete_priority(exit_mode_behavior)?;
 774:         }
 775:         inner
 776:             .exit_mode_mutations
 777:             .push(TunExitModeMutation::DeletePriority);
 778: 
 779:         match mode {
 780:             ExitMode::Off => {
 781:                 inner.current_exit_mode = ExitMode::Off;
 782:                 Ok(())
 783:             }
 784:             ExitMode::FullTunnel => {
 785:                 if failures_enabled {
 786:                     maybe_fail_exit_mode_add_priority(exit_mode_behavior)?;
 787:                 }
 788:                 inner
 789:                     .exit_mode_mutations
 790:                     .push(TunExitModeMutation::AddPriority);
 791:                 inner.current_exit_mode = ExitMode::FullTunnel;
 792:                 Ok(())
 793:             }
 794:         }
 795:     }
 796: 
 797:     #[cfg_attr(not(test), allow(dead_code))]
 798:     pub(crate) fn snapshot(&self) -> TunTestSnapshot {
 799:         let inner = self.inner.lock().expect("tun test state mutex poisoned");
 800:         TunTestSnapshot {
 801:             prepare_calls: inner.prepare_calls,
 802:             cleanup_calls: inner.cleanup_calls,
 803:             live_handles: inner.live_handles,
 804:             last_interface_name: inner.last_interface_name.clone(),
 805:             last_local_cidr: inner.last_local_cidr.clone(),
 806:             last_cleanup_interface_name: inner.last_cleanup_interface_name.clone(),
 807:             route_reconcile_calls: inner.route_reconcile_calls,
 808:             last_route_interface_name: inner.last_route_interface_name.clone(),
 809:             programmed_route_cidrs: inner.programmed_route_cidrs.clone(),
 810:             route_mutations: inner.route_mutations.clone(),
 811:             exit_mode_reconcile_calls: inner.exit_mode_reconcile_calls,
 812:             last_exit_mode_target: inner.last_exit_mode_target,
 813:             current_exit_mode: inner.current_exit_mode,
 814:             exit_mode_mutations: inner.exit_mode_mutations.clone(),
 815:         }
 816:     }
 817: }

 917:         Self { state }
 918:     }

 923:     }

 946: 
 947: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
 948: struct ParsedLocalCidr {
 949:     address: Ipv4Addr,
 950:     prefix_len: u8,
 951: }
 952: 
 953: impl ParsedLocalCidr {
 954:     fn parse(value: &str) -> Result<Self, BackendError> {
 955:         let (address, prefix_len) = value.split_once('/').ok_or_else(|| {
 956:             BackendError::invalid_input(
 957:                 "linux userspace-shared local cidr must contain an address and prefix length",
 958:             )
 959:         })?;
 960:         let address = address.parse::<Ipv4Addr>().map_err(|_| {
 961:             BackendError::invalid_input(
 962:                 "linux userspace-shared backend currently requires an IPv4 local cidr",
 963:             )
 964:         })?;
 965:         let prefix_len = prefix_len.parse::<u8>().map_err(|_| {
 966:             BackendError::invalid_input(
 967:                 "linux userspace-shared local cidr prefix length must be numeric",
 968:             )
 969:         })?;
 970:         if prefix_len > 32 {
 971:             return Err(BackendError::invalid_input(
 972:                 "linux userspace-shared local cidr prefix length must be <= 32",
 973:             ));
 974:         }
 975:         Ok(Self {
 976:             address,
 977:             prefix_len,
 978:         })
 979:     }
 980: }
 981: 
 982: fn reconcile_backend_routes(
 983:     runner: &mut dyn WireguardCommandRunner,
 984:     interface_name: &str,
 985:     previous_routes: &[Route],
 986:     next_routes: &[Route],
 987: ) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 988:     validate_interface_name(interface_name)?;
 989:     validate_route_set(next_routes)?;
 990: 
 991:     let forward_result =
 992:         apply_backend_route_plan(runner, interface_name, previous_routes, next_routes);
 993:     match forward_result {
 994:         Ok(()) => Ok(()),
 995:         Err(err) => {
 996:             let rollback_result =
 997:                 apply_backend_route_plan(runner, interface_name, next_routes, previous_routes);
 998:             match rollback_result {
 999:                 Ok(()) => Err(err),
1000:                 Err(rollback_err) => Err(combine_route_reconciliation_error(err, rollback_err)),
1001:             }
1002:         }
1003:     }
1004: }
1005: 
1006: fn apply_backend_route_plan(
1007:     runner: &mut dyn WireguardCommandRunner,
1008:     interface_name: &str,
1009:     previous_routes: &[Route],
1010:     next_routes: &[Route],
1011: ) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
1012:     for route in next_routes {
1013:         if matches!(route.kind, RouteKind::ExitNodeDefault) {
1014:             continue;
1015:         }
1016:         runner.run(
1017:             "ip",
1018:             &[
1019:                 "route".to_string(),
1020:                 "replace".to_string(),
1021:                 route.destination_cidr.clone(),
1022:                 "dev".to_string(),
1023:                 interface_name.to_string(),
1024:             ],
1025:         )?;
1026:     }
1027: 
1028:     for route in previous_routes {
1029:         if matches!(route.kind, RouteKind::ExitNodeDefault) {
1030:             continue;
1031:         }
1032:         if next_routes.iter().any(|candidate| candidate == route) {
1033:             continue;
1034:         }
1035:         match runner.run(
1036:             "ip",
1037:             &[
1038:                 "route".to_string(),
1039:                 "del".to_string(),
1040:                 route.destination_cidr.clone(),
1041:                 "dev".to_string(),
1042:                 interface_name.to_string(),
1043:             ],
1044:         ) {
1045:             Ok(()) => {}
1046:             Err(err) if is_missing_route_error(&err) => {}
1047:             Err(err) => return Err(err),
1048:         }
1049:     }
1050: 
1051:     Ok(())
1052: }
1053: 
1054: fn validate_route_set(routes: &[Route]) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
1055:     for route in routes {
1056:         validate_route_cidr(&route.destination_cidr)?;
1057:     }
1058:     Ok(())
1059: }
1060: 
1061: fn validate_route_cidr(value: &str) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
1062:     if value.is_empty() || !value.contains('/') {
1063:         return Err(BackendError::invalid_input("invalid cidr value"));
1064:     }
1065:     if !value
1066:         .chars()
1067:         .all(|ch| ch.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || ch == '.' || ch == ':' || ch == '/')
1068:     {
1069:         return Err(BackendError::invalid_input(
1070:             "cidr contains invalid characters",
1071:         ));
1072:     }
1073:     Ok(())
1074: }
1075: 
1076: fn backend_programmed_route_cidrs(routes: &[Route]) -> Vec<String> {
1077:     routes
1078:         .iter()
1079:         .filter(|route| !matches!(route.kind, RouteKind::ExitNodeDefault))
1080:         .map(|route| route.destination_cidr.clone())
1081:         .collect()
1082: }
1083: 
1084: fn combine_route_reconciliation_error(
1085:     primary: BackendError,
1086:     rollback: BackendError,
1087: ) -> BackendError {
1088:     BackendError::internal(format!(
1089:         "{}; route rollback failed: {}",
1090:         primary.message, rollback.message
1091:     ))
1092: }
1093: 
1094: fn reconcile_backend_exit_mode(
1095:     runner: &mut dyn WireguardCommandRunner,
1096:     previous_mode: ExitMode,
1097:     next_mode: ExitMode,
1098: ) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
1099:     let forward_result = apply_backend_exit_mode_plan(runner, next_mode);
1100:     match forward_result {
1101:         Ok(()) => Ok(()),
1102:         Err(err) => {
1103:             let rollback_result = apply_backend_exit_mode_plan(runner, previous_mode);
1104:             match rollback_result {
1105:                 Ok(()) => Err(err),
1106:                 Err(rollback_err) => Err(combine_exit_mode_reconciliation_error(err, rollback_err)),
1107:             }
1108:         }
1109:     }
1110: }
1111: 
1112: fn apply_backend_exit_mode_plan(
1113:     runner: &mut dyn WireguardCommandRunner,
1114:     mode: ExitMode,
1115: ) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
1116:     clear_backend_exit_rules(runner)?;
1117:     match mode {
1118:         ExitMode::Off => Ok(()),
1119:         ExitMode::FullTunnel => runner.run(
1120:             "ip",
1121:             &[
1122:                 "rule".to_string(),
1123:                 "add".to_string(),
1124:                 "priority".to_string(),
1125:                 "10000".to_string(),
1126:                 "table".to_string(),
1127:                 "51820".to_string(),
1128:             ],
1129:         ),
1130:     }
1131: }
1132: 
1133: fn clear_backend_exit_rules(runner: &mut dyn WireguardCommandRunner) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
1134:     for _ in 0..64 {
1135:         match runner.run(
1136:             "ip",
1137:             &[
1138:                 "rule".to_string(),
1139:                 "del".to_string(),
1140:                 "table".to_string(),
1141:                 "51820".to_string(),
1142:             ],
1143:         ) {
1144:             Ok(()) => {}
1145:             Err(err) if is_missing_rule_error(&err) => break,
1146:             Err(err) => return Err(err),
1147:         }
1148:     }
1149: 
1150:     match runner.run(
1151:         "ip",
1152:         &[
1153:             "rule".to_string(),
1154:             "del".to_string(),
1155:             "priority".to_string(),
1156:             "10000".to_string(),
1157:             "table".to_string(),
1158:             "51820".to_string(),
1159:         ],
1160:     ) {
1161:         Ok(()) => Ok(()),
1162:         Err(err) if is_missing_rule_error(&err) => Ok(()),
1163:         Err(err) => Err(err),
1164:     }
1165: }
1166: 
1167: fn combine_exit_mode_reconciliation_error(
1168:     primary: BackendError,
1169:     rollback: BackendError,
1170: ) -> BackendError {
1171:     BackendError::internal(format!(
1172:         "{}; exit-mode rollback failed: {}",
1173:         primary.message, rollback.message
1174:     ))
1175: }
1176: 
1177: fn is_missing_interface_error(err: &BackendError) -> bool {
1178:     let message = err.message.to_ascii_lowercase();
1179:     message.contains("cannot find device")
1180:         || message.contains("does not exist")
1181:         || message.contains("no such device")
1182:         || message.contains("cannot find")
1183: }
1184: 
1185: fn is_missing_route_error(err: &BackendError) -> bool {
1186:     let message = err.message.to_ascii_lowercase();
1187:     message.contains("rtnetlink answers: no such process")
1188:         || message.contains("no such process")
1189:         || message.contains("no such file or directory")
1190: }
1191: 
1192: fn is_missing_rule_error(err: &BackendError) -> bool {
1193:     let message = err.message.to_ascii_lowercase();
1194:     message.contains("rtnetlink answers: no such process")
1195:         || message.contains("no such process")
1196:         || message.contains("no such file or directory")
1197: }
1198: 
1199: fn maybe_fail_route_replace(behavior: &TestRouteBehavior, cidr: &str) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
1200:     match behavior {
1201:         TestRouteBehavior::Succeed => Ok(()),
1202:         TestRouteBehavior::FailOnReplace {
1203:             cidr: target_cidr,
1204:             message,
1205:         } if target_cidr == cidr => Err(BackendError::internal(message.clone())),
1206:         TestRouteBehavior::FailOnDelete { .. } => Ok(()),
1207:         TestRouteBehavior::FailOnReplace { .. } => Ok(()),
1208:     }
1209: }
1210: 
1211: fn maybe_fail_route_delete(behavior: &TestRouteBehavior, cidr: &str) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
1212:     match behavior {
1213:         TestRouteBehavior::Succeed => Ok(()),
1214:         TestRouteBehavior::FailOnDelete {
1215:             cidr: target_cidr,
1216:             message,
1217:         } if target_cidr == cidr => Err(BackendError::internal(message.clone())),
1218:         TestRouteBehavior::FailOnReplace { .. } => Ok(()),
1219:         TestRouteBehavior::FailOnDelete { .. } => Ok(()),
1220:     }
1221: }
1222: 
1223: fn maybe_fail_exit_mode_delete_table(behavior: &TestExitModeBehavior) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
1224:     match behavior {
1225:         TestExitModeBehavior::Succeed => Ok(()),
1226:         TestExitModeBehavior::FailOnDeleteTable { message } => {
1227:             Err(BackendError::internal(message.clone()))
1228:         }
1229:         TestExitModeBehavior::FailOnDeletePriority { .. }
1230:         | TestExitModeBehavior::FailOnAddPriority { .. } => Ok(()),
1231:     }
1232: }
1233: 
1234: fn maybe_fail_exit_mode_delete_priority(
1235:     behavior: &TestExitModeBehavior,
1236: ) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
1237:     match behavior {
1238:         TestExitModeBehavior::Succeed => Ok(()),
1239:         TestExitModeBehavior::FailOnDeletePriority { message } => {
1240:             Err(BackendError::internal(message.clone()))
1241:         }
1242:         TestExitModeBehavior::FailOnDeleteTable { .. }
1243:         | TestExitModeBehavior::FailOnAddPriority { .. } => Ok(()),
1244:     }
1245: }
1246: 
1247: fn maybe_fail_exit_mode_add_priority(behavior: &TestExitModeBehavior) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
1248:     match behavior {
1249:         TestExitModeBehavior::Succeed => Ok(()),
1250:         TestExitModeBehavior::FailOnAddPriority { message } => {
1251:             Err(BackendError::internal(message.clone()))
1252:         }
1253:         TestExitModeBehavior::FailOnDeleteTable { .. }
1254:         | TestExitModeBehavior::FailOnDeletePriority { .. } => Ok(()),
1255:     }
1256: }

1297:             let is_delete_table = program == "ip"
1298:                 && args

1300:                         .iter()

  44:         runner: R,
  45:         tunnel_name: impl Into<String>,
  46:         config_path: impl Into<String>,
  47:         private_key_path: impl Into<String>,
  48:         wireguard_exe_path: impl Into<String>,
  49:         wg_exe_path: impl Into<String>,
  50:         netsh_exe_path: impl Into<String>,
  51:         listen_port: u16,
  52:     ) -> Result<Self, BackendError> {
  53:         let tunnel_name = tunnel_name.into();
  54:         let config_path = PathBuf::from(config_path.into());
  55:         let private_key_path = PathBuf::from(private_key_path.into());
  56:         let wireguard_exe_path = PathBuf::from(wireguard_exe_path.into());

  59: 
  60:         validate_windows_tunnel_name(&tunnel_name)?;
  61:         validate_windows_config_path(config_path.as_path(), tunnel_name.as_str())?;
  62:         validate_host_absolute_path(private_key_path.as_path(), "windows private key path")?;
  63:         validate_host_absolute_path(wireguard_exe_path.as_path(), "windows wireguard.exe path")?;
  64:         validate_host_absolute_path(wg_exe_path.as_path(), "windows wg.exe path")?;
  65:         validate_host_absolute_path(netsh_exe_path.as_path(), "windows netsh.exe path")?;
  66:         validate_listen_port(listen_port)?;
  67: 
  68:         Ok(Self {
  69:             runner,
  70:             tunnel_name,
  71:             config_path,
  72:             private_key_path,
  73:             wireguard_exe_path,
  74:             wg_exe_path,

  76:             listen_port,
  77:             running: false,
  78:             peers: BTreeMap::new(),

  80:             context: None,
  81:             exit_mode: ExitMode::Off,
  82:         })
  83:     }

  85:     fn ensure_running(&self) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
  86:         if self.running {
  87:             return Ok(());
  88:         }
  89:         Err(BackendError::not_running(
  90:             "windows wireguard backend is not running",
  91:         ))
  92:     }
  93: 
  94:     fn ensure_prerequisites(&self) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
  95:         ensure_host_file_exists(self.private_key_path.as_path(), "windows private key file")?;
  96:         ensure_host_file_exists(
  97:             self.wireguard_exe_path.as_path(),
  98:             "windows wireguard.exe binary",
  99:         )?;
 100:         ensure_host_file_exists(self.wg_exe_path.as_path(), "windows wg.exe binary")?;
 101:         ensure_host_file_exists(self.netsh_exe_path.as_path(), "windows netsh.exe binary")
 102:     }

 104:     fn sync_persistent_config(&self) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 105:         let bytes = protect_config_bytes(
 106:             self.render_config()?.as_bytes(),
 107:             self.tunnel_name.as_str(),
 108:             self.config_path.as_path(),
 109:         )?;
 110:         write_config_atomically(self.config_path.as_path(), &bytes)

 113:     fn install_tunnel_service(&mut self) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 114:         self.runner.run(
 115:             self.wireguard_exe_path.to_string_lossy().as_ref(),
 116:             &[

 118:                 self.config_path.display().to_string(),

 123:     fn uninstall_tunnel_service(&mut self) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 124:         self.runner.run(
 125:             self.wireguard_exe_path.to_string_lossy().as_ref(),
 126:             &[
 127:                 "/uninstalltunnelservice".to_string(),
 128:                 self.tunnel_name.clone(),
 129:             ],

 132: 
 133:     fn apply_route_reconciliation(
 134:         &mut self,
 135:         next_routes: &[Route],
 136:         next_exit_mode: ExitMode,
 137:     ) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 138:         let current = effective_routes(&self.routes, self.exit_mode);
 139:         let next = effective_routes(next_routes, next_exit_mode);
 140: 
 141:         for destination_cidr in current.difference(&next) {
 142:             self.delete_os_route(destination_cidr)?;
 143:         }
 144:         for destination_cidr in next.difference(&current) {
 145:             self.add_os_route(destination_cidr)?;
 146:         }
 147: 
 148:         Ok(())
 149:     }
 150: 
 151:     fn add_os_route(&mut self, destination_cidr: &str) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 152:         validate_cidr(destination_cidr)?;
 153:         let (family, next_hop) = route_family_and_next_hop(destination_cidr)?;
 154:         self.runner.run(
 155:             self.netsh_exe_path.to_string_lossy().as_ref(),
 156:             &[
 157:                 "interface".to_string(),
 158:                 family.to_string(),
 159:                 "add".to_string(),
 160:                 "route".to_string(),
 161:                 format!("prefix={destination_cidr}"),
 162:                 format!("interface={}", self.tunnel_name),
 163:                 next_hop.to_string(),
 164:                 "store=active".to_string(),
 165:             ],
 166:         )
 167:     }
 168: 
 169:     fn delete_os_route(&mut self, destination_cidr: &str) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 170:         validate_cidr(destination_cidr)?;
 171:         let (family, _) = route_family_and_next_hop(destination_cidr)?;
 172:         self.runner.run(
 173:             self.netsh_exe_path.to_string_lossy().as_ref(),
 174:             &[
 175:                 "interface".to_string(),
 176:                 family.to_string(),
 177:                 "delete".to_string(),
 178:                 "route".to_string(),
 179:                 format!("prefix={destination_cidr}"),
 180:                 format!("interface={}", self.tunnel_name),
 181:                 "store=active".to_string(),
 182:             ],
 183:         )
 184:     }
 185: 
 186:     fn render_config(&self) -> Result<String, BackendError> {
 187:         let context = self.context.as_ref().ok_or_else(|| {
 188:             BackendError::not_running("windows wireguard backend has no runtime context")
 189:         })?;
 190:         validate_cidr(context.local_cidr.as_str())?;
 191:         let private_key = read_private_key_value(self.private_key_path.as_path())?;
 192:         let mut rendered = String::new();
 193:         rendered.push_str("[Interface]\n");
 194:         rendered.push_str("PrivateKey = ");
 195:         rendered.push_str(private_key.as_str());
 196:         rendered.push('\n');
 197:         rendered.push_str("Address = ");
 198:         rendered.push_str(context.local_cidr.as_str());
 199:         rendered.push('\n');
 200:         rendered.push_str("ListenPort = ");
 201:         rendered.push_str(&self.listen_port.to_string());
 202:         rendered.push('\n');
 203:         rendered.push('\n');
 204: 
 205:         for peer in self.peers.values() {
 206:             let allowed_ips = self.merged_allowed_ips(peer)?;
 207:             let endpoint = render_endpoint(peer.endpoint);
 208:             rendered.push_str("[Peer]\n");
 209:             rendered.push_str("PublicKey = ");
 210:             rendered.push_str(encode_wg_public_key_base64(&peer.public_key).as_str());
 211:             rendered.push('\n');
 212:             rendered.push_str("Endpoint = ");
 213:             rendered.push_str(endpoint.as_str());
 214:             rendered.push('\n');
 215:             rendered.push_str("AllowedIPs = ");
 216:             rendered.push_str(allowed_ips.as_str());
 217:             rendered.push('\n');
 218:             rendered.push('\n');
 219:         }
 220: 
 221:         Ok(rendered)
 222:     }
 223: 
 224:     fn merged_allowed_ips(&self, peer: &PeerConfig) -> Result<String, BackendError> {
 225:         if peer.allowed_ips.is_empty() {
 226:             return Err(BackendError::invalid_input(
 227:                 "peer allowed_ips must not be empty",
 228:             ));
 229:         }
 230:         let mut merged = BTreeSet::new();
 231:         for cidr in &peer.allowed_ips {
 232:             validate_cidr(cidr)?;
 233:             merged.insert(cidr.clone());
 234:         }
 235:         for route in self
 236:             .routes
 237:             .iter()
 238:             .filter(|route| route.via_node == peer.node_id)
 239:         {
 240:             validate_cidr(route.destination_cidr.as_str())?;
 241:             merged.insert(route.destination_cidr.clone());
 242:         }
 243:         if matches!(self.exit_mode, ExitMode::Off) {
 244:             merged.retain(|cidr| cidr != "0.0.0.0/0" && cidr != "::/0");
 245:             for cidr in &peer.allowed_ips {
 246:                 merged.insert(cidr.clone());
 247:             }
 248:         }
 249:         Ok(merged.into_iter().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(","))
 250:     }
 251: 
 252:     fn apply_peer_runtime(&mut self, peer: &PeerConfig) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 253:         let allowed_ips = self.merged_allowed_ips(peer)?;
 254:         let endpoint = render_endpoint(peer.endpoint);
 255:         self.runner.run(
 256:             self.wg_exe_path.to_string_lossy().as_ref(),
 257:             &[
 258:                 "set".to_string(),
 259:                 self.tunnel_name.clone(),
 260:                 "peer".to_string(),
 261:                 encode_wg_public_key_base64(&peer.public_key),
 262:                 "endpoint".to_string(),
 263:                 endpoint,
 264:                 "allowed-ips".to_string(),
 265:                 allowed_ips,
 266:             ],
 267:         )
 268:     }
 269: 
 270:     fn rewrite_runtime_peers(&mut self) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 271:         let peers = self.peers.values().cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>();
 272:         for peer in peers {
 273:             self.apply_peer_runtime(&peer)?;
 274:         }
 275:         Ok(())
 276:     }
 277: 
 278:     fn read_peer_latest_handshake_unix(
 279:         &mut self,
 280:         node_id: &NodeId,
 281:     ) -> Result<Option<u64>, BackendError> {
 282:         let peer = self
 283:             .peers
 284:             .get(node_id)
 285:             .ok_or_else(|| BackendError::invalid_input("peer is not configured"))?;
 286:         let output = self.runner.run_capture(
 287:             self.wg_exe_path.to_string_lossy().as_ref(),
 288:             &[
 289:                 "show".to_string(),
 290:                 self.tunnel_name.clone(),
 291:                 "latest-handshakes".to_string(),
 292:             ],
 293:         )?;
 294:         let public_key = encode_wg_public_key_base64(&peer.public_key);
 295:         parse_peer_latest_handshake_unix(&output.stdout, &public_key, self.peers.len().max(1))
 296:     }
 297: 
 298:     fn read_transfer_totals(&mut self) -> Result<(u64, u64), BackendError> {
 299:         let output = self.runner.run_capture(
 300:             self.wg_exe_path.to_string_lossy().as_ref(),
 301:             &[
 302:                 "show".to_string(),
 303:                 self.tunnel_name.clone(),
 304:                 "transfer".to_string(),
 305:             ],
 306:         )?;
 307:         parse_transfer_totals(&output, self.peers.values())
 308:     }
 309: }
 310: 
 311: impl<R: WireguardCommandRunner + Send + Sync + Clone> TunnelBackend for WindowsWireguardBackend<R> {
 312:     fn name(&self) -> &'static str {
 313:         "wireguard-windows"
 314:     }
 315: 
 316:     fn capabilities(&self) -> BackendCapabilities {
 317:         BackendCapabilities {
 318:             supports_roaming: true,
 319:             supports_exit_nodes: true,
 320:             supports_lan_routes: true,
 321:             supports_ipv6: true,
 322:         }
 323:     }
 324: 
 325:     fn start(&mut self, context: RuntimeContext) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 326:         if self.running {
 327:             return Err(BackendError::already_running(
 328:                 "windows wireguard backend already started",
 329:             ));
 330:         }
 331:         if context.interface_name != self.tunnel_name {
 332:             return Err(BackendError::invalid_input(
 333:                 "windows wireguard tunnel name must match runtime interface name",
 334:             ));
 335:         }
 336:         self.ensure_prerequisites()?;
 337:         self.context = Some(context);
 338:         self.sync_persistent_config()?;
 339:         if let Err(err) = self.install_tunnel_service() {
 340:             self.context = None;
 341:             return Err(err);
 342:         }
 343:         self.running = true;
 344:         Ok(())
 345:     }
 346: 
 347:     fn configure_peer(&mut self, peer: PeerConfig) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 348:         self.ensure_running()?;
 349:         let peer_for_runtime = peer.clone();
 350:         self.peers.insert(peer.node_id.clone(), peer);
 351:         self.apply_peer_runtime(&peer_for_runtime)?;
 352:         self.sync_persistent_config()
 353:     }
 354: 
 355:     fn update_peer_endpoint(
 356:         &mut self,
 357:         node_id: &NodeId,
 358:         endpoint: SocketEndpoint,
 359:     ) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 360:         self.ensure_running()?;
 361:         let Some(peer) = self.peers.get_mut(node_id) else {
 362:             return Err(BackendError::invalid_input("peer is not configured"));
 363:         };
 364:         peer.endpoint = endpoint;
 365:         let peer_for_runtime = peer.clone();
 366:         self.apply_peer_runtime(&peer_for_runtime)?;
 367:         self.sync_persistent_config()
 368:     }
 369: 
 370:     fn current_peer_endpoint(
 371:         &self,
 372:         node_id: &NodeId,
 373:     ) -> Result<Option<SocketEndpoint>, BackendError> {
 374:         self.ensure_running()?;
 375:         Ok(self.peers.get(node_id).map(|peer| peer.endpoint))
 376:     }
 377: 
 378:     fn peer_latest_handshake_unix(
 379:         &mut self,
 380:         node_id: &NodeId,
 381:     ) -> Result<Option<u64>, BackendError> {
 382:         self.ensure_running()?;
 383:         self.read_peer_latest_handshake_unix(node_id)
 384:     }
 385: 
 386:     fn remove_peer(&mut self, node_id: &NodeId) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 387:         self.ensure_running()?;
 388:         let Some(peer) = self.peers.remove(node_id) else {
 389:             return Ok(());
 390:         };
 391:         self.runner.run(
 392:             self.wg_exe_path.to_string_lossy().as_ref(),
 393:             &[
 394:                 "set".to_string(),
 395:                 self.tunnel_name.clone(),
 396:                 "peer".to_string(),
 397:                 encode_wg_public_key_base64(&peer.public_key),
 398:                 "remove".to_string(),
 399:             ],
 400:         )?;
 401:         self.sync_persistent_config()
 402:     }
 403: 
 404:     fn apply_routes(&mut self, routes: Vec<Route>) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 405:         self.ensure_running()?;
 406:         for route in &routes {
 407:             validate_cidr(route.destination_cidr.as_str())?;
 408:             if !self.peers.contains_key(&route.via_node) {
 409:                 return Err(BackendError::invalid_input(
 410:                     "route via_node must reference a configured peer",
 411:                 ));
 412:             }
 413:         }
 414:         self.apply_route_reconciliation(&routes, self.exit_mode)?;
 415:         self.routes = routes;
 416:         self.rewrite_runtime_peers()?;
 417:         self.sync_persistent_config()
 418:     }
 419: 
 420:     fn set_exit_mode(&mut self, mode: ExitMode) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 421:         self.ensure_running()?;
 422:         self.apply_route_reconciliation(&self.routes.clone(), mode)?;
 423:         self.exit_mode = mode;
 424:         self.rewrite_runtime_peers()?;
 425:         self.sync_persistent_config()
 426:     }
 427: 
 428:     fn stats(&self) -> Result<TunnelStats, BackendError> {
 429:         self.ensure_running()?;
 430:         let mut cloned = self.clone_for_stats()?;
 431:         let (bytes_rx, bytes_tx) = cloned.read_transfer_totals()?;
 432:         Ok(TunnelStats {
 433:             peer_count: self.peers.len(),
 434:             bytes_tx,
 435:             bytes_rx,
 436:             using_relay_path: false,
 437:         })
 438:     }
 439: 
 440:     fn transport_socket_identity_blocker(&self) -> Option<String> {
 441:         Some(
 442:             "windows wireguard backend is a command-only adapter over the official WireGuard for Windows tunnel service and its OS-managed WireGuardNT UDP socket; it exposes configuration and handshake queries but no authoritative packet-I/O handle or backend-owned datagram multiplexer, so the daemon cannot safely run STUN or relay bootstrap/refresh on the real peer-traffic transport, and a same-port daemon side socket is not authoritative transport identity".to_string(),
 443:         )
 444:     }
 445: 
 446:     fn shutdown(&mut self) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 447:         self.ensure_running()?;
 448:         let route_cleanup = self.apply_route_reconciliation(&[], ExitMode::Off);
 449:         let uninstall_result = self.uninstall_tunnel_service();
 450:         let cleanup_result = remove_file_if_present(self.config_path.as_path());
 451:         self.running = false;
 452:         self.peers.clear();
 453:         self.routes.clear();
 454:         self.context = None;
 455:         self.exit_mode = ExitMode::Off;
 456:         route_cleanup?;
 457:         uninstall_result?;
 458:         cleanup_result?;
 459:         Ok(())
 460:     }
 461: }
 462: 
 463: impl<R: WireguardCommandRunner + Clone> WindowsWireguardBackend<R> {
 464:     fn clone_for_stats(&self) -> Result<Self, BackendError> {
 465:         Ok(Self {
 466:             runner: self.runner.clone(),
 467:             tunnel_name: self.tunnel_name.clone(),
 468:             config_path: self.config_path.clone(),
 469:             private_key_path: self.private_key_path.clone(),
 470:             wireguard_exe_path: self.wireguard_exe_path.clone(),
 471:             wg_exe_path: self.wg_exe_path.clone(),
 472:             netsh_exe_path: self.netsh_exe_path.clone(),
 473:             listen_port: self.listen_port,
 474:             running: self.running,
 475:             peers: self.peers.clone(),
 476:             routes: self.routes.clone(),
 477:             context: self.context.clone(),
 478:             exit_mode: self.exit_mode,
 479:         })
 480:     }
 481: }
 482: 
 483: fn remove_file_if_present(path: &Path) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 484:     match fs::remove_file(path) {
 485:         Ok(()) => Ok(()),
 486:         Err(err) if err.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => Ok(()),
 487:         Err(err) => Err(BackendError::internal(format!(
 488:             "remove Windows tunnel config failed ({}): {err}",
 489:             path.display()
 490:         ))),
 491:     }
 492: }
 493: 
 494: fn write_config_atomically(path: &Path, bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 495:     let parent = path.parent().ok_or_else(|| {
 496:         BackendError::invalid_input(format!(
 497:             "windows config path must include a parent directory: {}",
 498:             path.display()
 499:         ))
 500:     })?;
 501:     fs::create_dir_all(parent).map_err(|err| {
 502:         BackendError::internal(format!(
 503:             "create Windows config directory failed ({}): {err}",
 504:             parent.display()
 505:         ))
 506:     })?;
 507:     let nanos = SystemTime::now()
 508:         .duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)
 509:         .map(|value| value.as_nanos())
 510:         .unwrap_or(0);
 511:     let temp_path = path.with_extension(format!("tmp.{}.{}", std::process::id(), nanos));
 512:     fs::write(&temp_path, bytes).map_err(|err| {
 513:         BackendError::internal(format!(
 514:             "write Windows tunnel config staging file failed ({}): {err}",
 515:             temp_path.display()
 516:         ))
 517:     })?;
 518:     fs::rename(&temp_path, path).map_err(|err| {
 519:         let _ = fs::remove_file(&temp_path);
 520:         BackendError::internal(format!(
 521:             "persist Windows tunnel config failed ({}): {err}",
 522:             path.display()
 523:         ))
 524:     })
 525: }
 526: 
 527: fn protect_config_bytes(
 528:     bytes: &[u8],
 529:     tunnel_name: &str,
 530:     path: &Path,
 531: ) -> Result<Vec<u8>, BackendError> {
 532:     #[cfg(windows)]
 533:     {
 534:         return dpapi_protect(bytes, WindowsDpapiScope::CurrentUser, tunnel_name).map_err(|err| {
 535:             BackendError::internal(format!(
 536:                 "DPAPI protect failed for Windows tunnel config {}: {err}",
 537:                 path.display()
 538:             ))
 539:         });
 540:     }
 541:     #[cfg(not(windows))]
 542:     {
 543:         let _ = tunnel_name;
 544:         let _ = path;
 545:         Ok(bytes.to_vec())
 546:     }
 547: }
 548: 
 549: fn read_private_key_value(path: &Path) -> Result<String, BackendError> {
 550:     let contents = fs::read_to_string(path).map_err(|err| {
 551:         BackendError::internal(format!(
 552:             "read Windows private key file failed ({}): {err}",
 553:             path.display()
 554:         ))
 555:     })?;
 556:     let key = contents.trim();
 557:     if key.is_empty() {
 558:         return Err(BackendError::invalid_input(
 559:             "windows private key file must not be empty",
 560:         ));
 561:     }
 562:     if !key
 563:         .chars()
 564:         .all(|ch| ch.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || matches!(ch, '+' | '/' | '='))
 565:     {
 566:         return Err(BackendError::invalid_input(
 567:             "windows private key file contains invalid characters",
 568:         ));
 569:     }
 570:     Ok(key.to_string())
 571: }
 572: 
 573: fn parse_transfer_totals(
 574:     output: &WireguardCommandOutput,
 575:     peers: impl Iterator<Item = impl std::borrow::Borrow<PeerConfig>>,
 576: ) -> Result<(u64, u64), BackendError> {
 577:     let allowed_public_keys = peers
 578:         .map(|peer| encode_wg_public_key_base64(&peer.borrow().public_key))
 579:         .collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
 580:     let mut bytes_rx = 0u64;
 581:     let mut bytes_tx = 0u64;
 582:     for line in output.stdout.lines() {
 583:         let trimmed = line.trim();
 584:         if trimmed.is_empty() {
 585:             continue;
 586:         }
 587:         let fields = trimmed.split_whitespace().collect::<Vec<_>>();
 588:         if fields.len() != 3 {
 589:             return Err(BackendError::internal(
 590:                 "wg show transfer produced an unexpected row shape",
 591:             ));
 592:         }
 593:         if !allowed_public_keys.contains(fields[0]) {
 594:             continue;
 595:         }
 596:         let peer_rx = fields[1].parse::<u64>().map_err(|_| {
 597:             BackendError::internal("wg show transfer produced a non-numeric rx byte count")
 598:         })?;
 599:         let peer_tx = fields[2].parse::<u64>().map_err(|_| {
 600:             BackendError::internal("wg show transfer produced a non-numeric tx byte count")
 601:         })?;
 602:         bytes_rx = bytes_rx.saturating_add(peer_rx);
 603:         bytes_tx = bytes_tx.saturating_add(peer_tx);
 604:     }
 605:     Ok((bytes_rx, bytes_tx))
 606: }
 607: 
 608: fn render_endpoint(endpoint: SocketEndpoint) -> String {
 609:     if endpoint.addr.is_ipv6() {
 610:         format!("[{}]:{}", endpoint.addr, endpoint.port)
 611:     } else {
 612:         format!("{}:{}", endpoint.addr, endpoint.port)
 613:     }
 614: }
 615: 
 616: fn validate_cidr(value: &str) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 617:     if value.is_empty() || !value.contains('/') {
 618:         return Err(BackendError::invalid_input("invalid cidr value"));
 619:     }
 620:     if !value
 621:         .chars()
 622:         .all(|ch| ch.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || matches!(ch, '.' | ':' | '/'))
 623:     {
 624:         return Err(BackendError::invalid_input(
 625:             "cidr contains invalid characters",
 626:         ));
 627:     }
 628:     Ok(())
 629: }
 630: 
 631: fn validate_windows_tunnel_name(name: &str) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 632:     const RESERVED_NAMES: [&str; 22] = [
 633:         "CON", "PRN", "AUX", "NUL", "COM1", "COM2", "COM3", "COM4", "COM5", "COM6", "COM7", "COM8",
 634:         "COM9", "LPT1", "LPT2", "LPT3", "LPT4", "LPT5", "LPT6", "LPT7", "LPT8", "LPT9",
 635:     ];
 636:     if name.is_empty() || name.len() > 32 {
 637:         return Err(BackendError::invalid_input(
 638:             "windows tunnel name length must be between 1 and 32",
 639:         ));
 640:     }
 641:     if RESERVED_NAMES
 642:         .iter()
 643:         .any(|candidate| candidate.eq_ignore_ascii_case(name))
 644:     {
 645:         return Err(BackendError::invalid_input(
 646:             "windows tunnel name must not use a reserved device name",
 647:         ));
 648:     }
 649:     if !name
 650:         .chars()
 651:         .all(|ch| ch.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || matches!(ch, '_' | '=' | '+' | '.' | '-'))
 652:     {
 653:         return Err(BackendError::invalid_input(
 654:             "windows tunnel name contains invalid characters",
 655:         ));
 656:     }
 657:     Ok(())
 658: }
 659: 
 660: fn validate_windows_config_path(path: &Path, tunnel_name: &str) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 661:     validate_host_absolute_path(path, "windows tunnel config path")?;
 662:     let file_name = path
 663:         .file_name()
 664:         .and_then(|value| value.to_str())
 665:         .ok_or_else(|| {
 666:             BackendError::invalid_input("windows tunnel config path must end in a file name")
 667:         })?;
 668:     let expected_name = format!("{tunnel_name}{WINDOWS_CONFIG_FILE_SUFFIX}");
 669:     if file_name != expected_name {
 670:         return Err(BackendError::invalid_input(format!(
 671:             "windows tunnel config file name must be {expected_name}"
 672:         )));
 673:     }
 674:     Ok(())
 675: }
 676: 
 677: fn ensure_host_file_exists(path: &Path, label: &str) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 678:     let metadata = fs::metadata(path).map_err(|err| {
 679:         BackendError::invalid_input(format!(
 680:             "{label} is missing or unreadable ({}): {err}",
 681:             path.display()
 682:         ))
 683:     })?;
 684:     if !metadata.is_file() {
 685:         return Err(BackendError::invalid_input(format!(
 686:             "{label} must be a regular file: {}",
 687:             path.display()
 688:         )));
 689:     }
 690:     Ok(())
 691: }
 692: 
 693: fn effective_routes(routes: &[Route], exit_mode: ExitMode) -> BTreeSet<String> {
 694:     routes
 695:         .iter()
 696:         .filter(|route| {
 697:             matches!(exit_mode, ExitMode::FullTunnel)
 698:                 || !is_default_route(route.destination_cidr.as_str())
 699:         })
 700:         .map(|route| route.destination_cidr.clone())
 701:         .collect()
 702: }
 703: 
 704: fn is_default_route(destination_cidr: &str) -> bool {
 705:     matches!(destination_cidr, "0.0.0.0/0" | "::/0")
 706: }
 707: 
 708: fn route_family_and_next_hop(
 709:     destination_cidr: &str,
 710: ) -> Result<(&'static str, &'static str), BackendError> {
 711:     if destination_cidr.contains(':') {
 712:         return Ok(("ipv6", "nexthop=::"));
 713:     }
 714:     Ok(("ipv4", "nexthop=0.0.0.0"))
 715: }
 716: 
 717: fn validate_host_absolute_path(path: &Path, label: &str) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 718:     #[cfg(windows)]
 719:     {
 720:         let text = path.to_string_lossy().replace('/', "\\");
 721:         let bytes = text.as_bytes();
 722:         if bytes.len() < 3
 723:             || !bytes[0].is_ascii_alphabetic()
 724:             || bytes[1] != b':'
 725:             || bytes[2] != b'\\'
 726:         {
 727:             return Err(BackendError::invalid_input(format!(
 728:                 "{label} must be an absolute Windows path"
 729:             )));
 730:         }
 731:     }
 732:     #[cfg(not(windows))]
 733:     {
 734:         if !path.is_absolute() {
 735:             return Err(BackendError::invalid_input(format!(
 736:                 "{label} must be absolute"
 737:             )));
 738:         }
 739:     }
 740:     Ok(())
 741: }

 743: #[cfg(test)]
 744: mod tests {
 745:     use super::*;
 746:     use base64::prelude::*;
 747:     use rustynet_backend_api::RouteKind;
 748:     use std::sync::{Arc, Mutex};
 749:     use tempfile::TempDir;
 750: 
 751:     #[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
 752:     struct RecordingRunner {
 753:         #[allow(clippy::type_complexity)]
 754:         commands: Arc<Mutex<Vec<(String, Vec<String>)>>>,
 755:         handshake_output: Arc<Mutex<String>>,
 756:         transfer_output: Arc<Mutex<String>>,
 757:     }
 758: 
 759:     impl RecordingRunner {
 760:         fn recorded(&self) -> Vec<(String, Vec<String>)> {
 761:             self.commands.lock().expect("commands").clone()
 762:         }
 763:     }
 764: 
 765:     impl WireguardCommandRunner for RecordingRunner {
 766:         fn run(&mut self, program: &str, args: &[String]) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
 767:             self.commands
 768:                 .lock()
 769:                 .expect("commands")
 770:                 .push((program.to_string(), args.to_vec()));
 771:             Ok(())
 772:         }
 773: 
 774:         fn run_capture(
 775:             &mut self,
 776:             program: &str,
 777:             args: &[String],
 778:         ) -> Result<WireguardCommandOutput, BackendError> {
 779:             self.commands
 780:                 .lock()
 781:                 .expect("commands")
 782:                 .push((program.to_string(), args.to_vec()));
 783:             let stdout = if args.iter().any(|arg| arg == "latest-handshakes") {
 784:                 self.handshake_output.lock().expect("handshake").clone()
 785:             } else if args.iter().any(|arg| arg == "transfer") {
 786:                 self.transfer_output.lock().expect("transfer").clone()
 787:             } else {
 788:                 String::new()
 789:             };
 790:             Ok(WireguardCommandOutput {
 791:                 stdout,
 792:                 stderr: String::new(),
 793:             })
 794:         }
 795:     }
 796: 
 797:     fn runtime_context() -> RuntimeContext {
 798:         RuntimeContext {
 799:             local_node: NodeId::new("local-node").expect("valid node id"),
 800:             interface_name: "rustynet0".to_string(),
 801:             mesh_cidr: "100.64.0.0/10".to_string(),
 802:             local_cidr: "100.64.0.1/32".to_string(),
 803:         }
 804:     }
 805:

 807:         PeerConfig {
 808:             node_id: NodeId::new(name).expect("valid node id"),
 809:             endpoint: SocketEndpoint {
 810:                 addr: "203.0.113.10".parse().expect("valid ip"),
 811:                 port: 51820,
 812:             },
 813:             public_key: [7; 32],
 814:             allowed_ips: vec!["100.64.10.0/24".to_string()],
 815:         }
 816:     }

 818:     fn write_private_key(temp_dir: &TempDir) -> PathBuf {
 819:         let path = temp_dir.path().join("wireguard.key");
 820:         fs::write(&path, format!("{}\n", BASE64_STANDARD.encode([9u8; 32])))
 821:             .expect("private key should be written");
 822:         path
 823:     }
 824: 
 825:     fn backend_paths(temp_dir: &TempDir) -> (PathBuf, PathBuf, PathBuf, PathBuf, PathBuf) {
 826:         let config = temp_dir.path().join("rustynet0.conf.dpapi");
 827:         let private_key = write_private_key(temp_dir);
 828:         let wireguard = temp_dir.path().join("wireguard.exe");
 829:         let wg = temp_dir.path().join("wg.exe");
 830:         let netsh = temp_dir.path().join("netsh.exe");
 831:         fs::write(&wireguard, b"").expect("wireguard path should exist");
 832:         fs::write(&wg, b"").expect("wg path should exist");
 833:         fs::write(&netsh, b"").expect("netsh path should exist");
 834:         (config, private_key, wireguard, wg, netsh)
 835:     }

 838:     fn windows_backend_start_configures_tunnel_service_and_persists_dpapi_config_shape() {

 841:             backend_paths(&temp_dir);

 844:             runner.clone(),
 845:             "rustynet0",

 847:             private_key_path.to_string_lossy(),
 848:             wireguard_path.to_string_lossy(),
 849:             wg_path.to_string_lossy(),
 850:             netsh_path.to_string_lossy(),

 852:         )
 853:         .expect("backend should construct");
 854:

 856:             .start(runtime_context())
 857:             .expect("backend should start successfully");

 876:     #[test]
 877:     fn windows_backend_route_lifecycle_rewrites_allowed_ips_for_configured_peer() {
 878:         let temp_dir = TempDir::new().expect("temp dir");
 879:         let (config_path, private_key_path, wireguard_path, wg_path, netsh_path) =
 880:             backend_paths(&temp_dir);
 881:         let runner = RecordingRunner::default();
 882:         let mut backend = WindowsWireguardBackend::new(
 883:             runner.clone(),
 884:             "rustynet0",
 885:             config_path.to_string_lossy(),
 886:             private_key_path.to_string_lossy(),
 887:             wireguard_path.to_string_lossy(),
 888:             wg_path.to_string_lossy(),
 889:             netsh_path.to_string_lossy(),
 890:             51820,
 891:         )
 892:         .expect("backend should construct");
 893:         let peer = sample_peer("peer-a");
 894: 
 895:         backend
 896:             .start(runtime_context())
 897:             .expect("backend should start successfully");
 898:         backend
 899:             .configure_peer(peer.clone())
 900:             .expect("peer should configure");
 901:         backend
 902:             .apply_routes(vec![
 903:                 Route {
 904:                     destination_cidr: "100.64.20.0/24".to_string(),
 905:                     via_node: peer.node_id.clone(),
 906:                     kind: RouteKind::Mesh,
 907:                 },
 908:                 Route {
 909:                     destination_cidr: "0.0.0.0/0".to_string(),
 910:                     via_node: peer.node_id.clone(),
 911:                     kind: RouteKind::ExitNodeDefault,
 912:                 },
 913:             ])
 914:             .expect("routes should apply");
 915:         backend
 916:             .set_exit_mode(ExitMode::FullTunnel)
 917:             .expect("full-tunnel mode should apply");
 918: 
 919:         let written = fs::read_to_string(&config_path).expect("config should be readable");
 920:         assert!(written.contains("AllowedIPs = 0.0.0.0/0,100.64.10.0/24,100.64.20.0/24"));
 921:         let recorded = runner.recorded();
 922:         assert!(
 923:             recorded
 924:                 .iter()
 925:                 .any(|(_, args)| args.iter().any(|arg| arg == "allowed-ips")),
 926:             "runtime peer rewrite should issue wg set allowed-ips"
 927:         );
 928:         assert!(
 929:             recorded.iter().any(|(program, args)| {
 930:                 program == &netsh_path.to_string_lossy()
 931:                     && args
 932:                         == &vec![
 933:                             "interface".to_string(),
 934:                             "ipv4".to_string(),
 935:                             "add".to_string(),
 936:                             "route".to_string(),
 937:                             "prefix=100.64.20.0/24".to_string(),
 938:                             "interface=rustynet0".to_string(),
 939:                             "nexthop=0.0.0.0".to_string(),
 940:                             "store=active".to_string(),
 941:                         ]
 942:             }),
 943:             "mesh route should be installed through netsh"
 944:         );
 945:         assert!(
 946:             recorded.iter().any(|(program, args)| {
 947:                 program == &netsh_path.to_string_lossy()
 948:                     && args
 949:                         == &vec![
 950:                             "interface".to_string(),
 951:                             "ipv4".to_string(),
 952:                             "add".to_string(),
 953:                             "route".to_string(),
 954:                             "prefix=0.0.0.0/0".to_string(),
 955:                             "interface=rustynet0".to_string(),
 956:                             "nexthop=0.0.0.0".to_string(),
 957:                             "store=active".to_string(),
 958:                         ]
 959:             }),
 960:             "default route should be installed only after exit mode enables it"
 961:         );
 962:     }
 963: 
 964:     #[test]
 965:     fn windows_backend_stats_and_handshakes_are_read_from_wg_show() {
 966:         let temp_dir = TempDir::new().expect("temp dir");
 967:         let (config_path, private_key_path, wireguard_path, wg_path, netsh_path) =
 968:             backend_paths(&temp_dir);
 969:         let runner = RecordingRunner::default();
 970:         let peer = sample_peer("peer-a");
 971:         let peer_public_key = encode_wg_public_key_base64(&peer.public_key);
 972:         *runner.handshake_output.lock().expect("handshake") = format!("{peer_public_key}\t123\n");
 973:         *runner.transfer_output.lock().expect("transfer") = format!("{peer_public_key}\t11\t22\n");
 974: 
 975:         let mut backend = WindowsWireguardBackend::new(
 976:             runner.clone(),
 977:             "rustynet0",
 978:             config_path.to_string_lossy(),
 979:             private_key_path.to_string_lossy(),
 980:             wireguard_path.to_string_lossy(),
 981:             wg_path.to_string_lossy(),
 982:             netsh_path.to_string_lossy(),
 983:             51820,
 984:         )
 985:         .expect("backend should construct");
 986: 
 987:         backend
 988:             .start(runtime_context())
 989:             .expect("backend should start successfully");
 990:         backend
 991:             .configure_peer(peer.clone())
 992:             .expect("peer should configure");
 993: 
 994:         let stats = backend.stats().expect("stats should succeed");
 995:         assert_eq!(stats.peer_count, 1);
 996:         assert_eq!(stats.bytes_rx, 11);
 997:         assert_eq!(stats.bytes_tx, 22);
 998:         assert_eq!(
 999:             backend
1000:                 .peer_latest_handshake_unix(&peer.node_id)
1001:                 .expect("handshake lookup should succeed"),
1002:             Some(123)
1003:         );
1004:     }

1009:         let (config_path, private_key_path, wireguard_path, wg_path, netsh_path) =
1010:             backend_paths(&temp_dir);
1011:         let runner = RecordingRunner::default();

1013:             runner.clone(),
1014:             "rustynet0",
1015:             config_path.to_string_lossy(),
1016:             private_key_path.to_string_lossy(),
1017:             wireguard_path.to_string_lossy(),
1018:             wg_path.to_string_lossy(),
1019:             netsh_path.to_string_lossy(),
1020:             51820,
1021:         )
1022:         .expect("backend should construct");
1023: 
1024:         backend
1025:             .start(runtime_context())
1026:             .expect("backend should start successfully");
1027:         backend.shutdown().expect("shutdown should succeed");
1028:

1030:             !config_path.exists(),
1031:             "shutdown should remove persisted tunnel config"
1032:         );

1035:             recorded.last().expect("shutdown command").1,
1036:             vec![
1037:                 "/uninstalltunnelservice".to_string(),
1038:                 "rustynet0".to_string()
1039:             ]
1040:         );
1041:     }

===== /crates/rustynet-backend-wireguard/tests/conformance.rs =====

   1: #![forbid(unsafe_code)]
   2: 
   3: use rustynet_backend_api::{
   4:     BackendErrorKind, ExitMode, NodeId, PeerConfig, Route, RouteKind, RuntimeContext,
   5:     SocketEndpoint, TunnelBackend,
   6: };
   7: use rustynet_backend_wireguard::{
   8:     LinuxUserspaceSharedBackend, LinuxWireguardBackend, MacosWireguardBackend,
   9:     WindowsWireguardBackend, WireguardBackend, WireguardCommandOutput, WireguardCommandRunner,
  10: };
  11: use std::fs;
  12: use std::net::{SocketAddr, UdpSocket};
  13: use std::path::PathBuf;
  14: use std::time::{SystemTime, UNIX_EPOCH};
  15: 
  16: use base64::prelude::*;
  17: 
  18: fn runtime_context() -> RuntimeContext {
  19:     RuntimeContext {
  20:         local_node: NodeId::new("local-node").expect("valid node id"),
  21:         interface_name: "rustynet0".to_string(),
  22:         mesh_cidr: "100.64.0.0/10".to_string(),
  23:         local_cidr: "100.64.0.1/32".to_string(),
  24:     }
  25: }
  26: 
  27: fn sample_peer(name: &str) -> PeerConfig {
  28:     PeerConfig {
  29:         node_id: NodeId::new(name).expect("valid node id"),
  30:         endpoint: SocketEndpoint {
  31:             addr: "203.0.113.10".parse().expect("valid ip"),
  32:             port: 51820,
  33:         },
  34:         public_key: [7; 32],
  35:         allowed_ips: vec!["100.64.1.0/24".to_string()],
  36:     }
  37: }
  38: 
  39: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
  40: struct StubRunner;
  41: 
  42: impl WireguardCommandRunner for StubRunner {
  43:     fn run(
  44:         &mut self,
  45:         _program: &str,
  46:         _args: &[String],
  47:     ) -> Result<(), rustynet_backend_api::BackendError> {
  48:         Ok(())
  49:     }
  50: 
  51:     fn run_capture(
  52:         &mut self,
  53:         _program: &str,
  54:         _args: &[String],
  55:     ) -> Result<WireguardCommandOutput, rustynet_backend_api::BackendError> {
  56:         Ok(WireguardCommandOutput {
  57:             stdout: String::new(),
  58:             stderr: String::new(),
  59:         })
  60:     }
  61: }
  62: 
  63: fn unique_path(name: &str) -> PathBuf {
  64:     let nanos = SystemTime::now()
  65:         .duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)
  66:         .expect("time should move forward")
  67:         .as_nanos();
  68:     std::env::temp_dir().join(format!(
  69:         "rustynet-userspace-shared-conformance-{name}-{}-{nanos}.key",
  70:         std::process::id()
  71:     ))
  72: }
  73: 
  74: fn write_private_key(bytes: [u8; 32]) -> PathBuf {
  75:     let path = unique_path("valid");
  76:     fs::write(&path, format!("{}\n", BASE64_STANDARD.encode(bytes)))
  77:         .expect("private key should be written");
  78:     path
  79: }
  80: 
  81: fn free_listen_port() -> u16 {
  82:     let socket = UdpSocket::bind(SocketAddr::from(([0, 0, 0, 0], 0)))
  83:         .expect("ephemeral port should be available");
  84:     socket.local_addr().expect("local addr").port()
  85: }
  86: 
  87: #[test]
  88: fn wireguard_backend_follows_lifecycle_contract() {
  89:     let mut backend = WireguardBackend::default();
  90: 
  91:     let pre_start_err = backend.stats().expect_err("stats before start should fail");
  92:     assert_eq!(pre_start_err.kind, BackendErrorKind::NotRunning);
  93: 
  94:     backend
  95:         .start(runtime_context())
  96:         .expect("backend should start");
  97:     backend
  98:         .configure_peer(sample_peer("peer-a"))
  99:         .expect("peer config should succeed");
 100:     backend
 101:         .set_exit_mode(ExitMode::FullTunnel)
 102:         .expect("exit mode switch should succeed");
 103:     backend
 104:         .apply_routes(vec![Route {
 105:             destination_cidr: "0.0.0.0/0".to_string(),
 106:             via_node: NodeId::new("peer-a").expect("valid node id"),
 107:             kind: RouteKind::ExitNodeDefault,
 108:         }])
 109:         .expect("route apply should succeed");
 110: 
 111:     let stats = backend.stats().expect("stats should succeed");
 112:     assert_eq!(stats.peer_count, 1);
 113: 
 114:     backend.shutdown().expect("shutdown should succeed");
 115: }

 117: #[test]
 118: fn wireguard_backend_rejects_double_start() {
 119:     let mut backend = WireguardBackend::default();
 120:     backend
 121:         .start(runtime_context())
 122:         .expect("backend should start");
 123:     let err = backend
 124:         .start(runtime_context())
 125:         .expect_err("second start should fail");
 126:     assert_eq!(err.kind, BackendErrorKind::AlreadyRunning);
 127: }

 129: #[test]
 130: fn wireguard_backend_remove_peer_updates_runtime_state() {
 131:     let mut backend = WireguardBackend::default();
 132:     backend
 133:         .start(runtime_context())
 134:         .expect("backend should start successfully");
 135:     backend
 136:         .configure_peer(sample_peer("peer-a"))
 137:         .expect("peer a should configure");
 138:     backend
 139:         .configure_peer(sample_peer("peer-b"))
 140:         .expect("peer b should configure");
 141: 
 142:     backend
 143:         .remove_peer(&NodeId::new("peer-a").expect("valid node id"))
 144:         .expect("peer removal should succeed");
